(3.38d)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-\textup{{Sym}}[{\cal B}_{1,0}P_{0}P_{0,2}+{\cal B}_{0,1}P_{0}P_{1,1}]\big)f_{k}^{\sigma}\,,

\displaystyle\textup{$\mathfrak{B}$}_{2,1}f_{k}^{\sigma}=P_{0}^{*}\big({\cal B}_{2,1}+{\cal B}_{1,1}P_{1,0}+{\cal B}_{2,0}P_{0,1}+{\cal B}_{0,1}P_{2,0}+{\cal B}_{1,0}P_{1,1}+\frac{1}{2}\Pi_{0}^{+}P_{2,1}

(3.38e)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-\textup{{Sym}}[{\cal B}_{0,1}P_{0}P_{2,0}+{\cal B}_{1,0}P_{0}P_{1,1}]\big)f_{k}^{\sigma}\,,

\displaystyle\textup{$\mathfrak{B}$}_{0,4}f_{k}^{\sigma}=P_{0}^{*}\big({\cal B}_{0,4}+{\cal B}_{0,3}P_{0,1}+{\cal B}_{0,2}P_{0,2}+{\cal B}_{0,1}P_{0,3}

(3.38f)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-\textup{{Sym}}[{\cal B}_{0,2}P_{0}P_{0,2}+{\cal B}_{0,1}P_{0}P_{0,3}+{\cal B}_{0,1}P_{0,1}P_{0}P_{0,2}-\mathfrak{N}_{0,2}P_{0}P_{0,2}]\big)f_{k}^{\sigma}\,,

\displaystyle\textup{$\mathfrak{B}$}_{2,2}f_{k}^{\sigma}=P_{0}^{*}\big({\cal B}_{2,2}+{\cal B}_{1,2}P_{1,0}+{\cal B}_{2,1}P_{0,1}+{\cal B}_{0,2}P_{2,0}+{\cal B}_{1,1}P_{1,1}+{\cal B}_{2,0}P_{0,2}+{\cal B}_{0,1}P_{2,1}+{\cal B}_{1,0}P_{1,2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\Pi_{0}^{+}P_{2,2}-\textup{{Sym}}[{\cal B}_{0,2}P_{0}P_{2,0}+{\cal B}_{1,1}P_{0}P_{1,1}+{\cal B}_{2,0}P_{0}P_{0,2}+{\cal B}_{0,1}P_{0}P_{2,1}+{\cal B}_{1,0}P_{0}P_{1,2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+{\cal B}_{0,1}P_{0,1}P_{0}P_{2,0}+{\cal B}_{1,0}P_{0,1}P_{0}P_{1,1}+{\cal B}_{0,1}P_{1,0}P_{0}P_{1,1}+{\cal B}_{1,0}P_{1,0}P_{0}P_{0,2}

(3.38g)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-\mathfrak{N}_{2,0}P_{0}P_{0,2}-\mathfrak{N}_{0,2}P_{0}P_{2,0}-\mathfrak{N}_{1,1}P_{0}P_{1,1}]\big)f_{k}^{\sigma}\,,

\displaystyle\textup{$\mathfrak{B}$}_{1,3}f_{k}^{\sigma}=P_{0}^{*}\big({\cal B}_{1,3}+{\cal B}_{0,3}P_{1,0}+{\cal B}_{1,2}P_{0,1}+{\cal B}_{0,2}P_{1,1}+{\cal B}_{1,1}P_{0,2}+{\cal B}_{1,0}P_{0,3}+{\cal B}_{0,1}P_{1,2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\Pi_{0}^{+}P_{1,3}-\textup{{Sym}}[{\cal B}_{0,2}P_{0}P_{1,1}+{\cal B}_{1,1}P_{0}P_{0,2}+{\cal B}_{1,0}P_{0}P_{0,3}+{\cal B}_{0,1}P_{0}P_{1,2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+{\cal B}_{1,0}P_{0,1}P_{0}P_{0,2}+{\cal B}_{0,1}P_{1,0}P_{0}P_{0,2}+{\cal B}_{0,1}P_{0,1}P_{0}P_{1,1}-\mathfrak{N}_{1,1}P_{0}P_{0,2}-\mathfrak{N}_{0,2}P_{0}P_{1,1}]\big)f_{k}^{\sigma}\,,

(3.38h)

with
{\cal B}_{j}
,
j=0,\dots,4
, in (
3.8
) and
P_{j}
,
j=0,\dots,3
, in (
3.13
).

The proof of (
3.38
) relies on formulas (
3.24a
)–(
3.24c
) and Lemmata
3.9
,
3.10
below.

Lemma 3.9
.

Let
f_{k}^{\sigma}\in\{f_{1}^{+},f_{1}^{-},f_{0}^{-}\}
. For any
j\in\mathbb{N}
we have

{\footnotesize\begin{matrix}P_{0}^{*}\textup{{Sym}}[{\cal B}_{j}+{\cal B}_{j-1}P_{1}+\dots+{\cal B}_{1}P_{j-1}]P_{0}f_{k}^{\sigma}=P_{0}^{*}\big({\cal B}_{j}+{\cal B}_{j-1}P_{1}+\dots+{\cal B}_{1}P_{j-1}+\frac{1}{2}\Pi_{0}^{+}P_{j}\big)P_{0}f_{k}^{\sigma}\end{matrix}}\,,

(3.39)

where
\Pi_{0}^{+}
is the orthogonal projector on
f_{0}^{+}
.

Proof.

By identity (
3.29
) the operator
{\cal B}_{\mu,\epsilon}P_{\mu,\epsilon}
is, like
{\cal B}_{\mu,\epsilon}
, self-adjoint, hence its
j
-th jet fulfills

\textup{{Sym}}[{\cal B}_{j}P_{0}+\dots+{\cal B}_{1}P_{j-1}]={\cal B}_{j}P_{0}+\dots+{\cal B}_{1}P_{j-1}+{\cal B}_{0}P_{j}-\textup{{Sym}}[{\cal B}_{0}P_{j}]\,.

(3.40)

We claim that, for
f_{k}^{\sigma}\in\{f_{1}^{+},f_{1}^{-},f_{0}^{-}\}
we have

P_{0}^{*}({\cal B}_{0}P_{j}-\mathbf{Sym}[{\cal B}_{0}P_{j}])P_{0}f_{k}^{\sigma}=\frac{1}{2}P_{0}^{*}\Pi_{0}^{+}P_{j}P_{0}f_{k}^{\sigma}\,,

(3.41)

which, together with (
3.40
), proves (
3.39
). Claim (
3.41
) follows, by observing that
f_{k}^{\sigma}
fulfills

{\cal B}_{0}f_{k}^{\sigma}=0
and
\Pi^{\angle}P_{0}f_{k}^{\sigma}=f_{k}^{\sigma}
(cfr. Lemma
3.7
), then

P_{0}^{*}\textup{{Sym}}[{\cal B}_{0}P_{j}]P_{0}f_{k}^{\sigma}=\frac{1}{2}P_{0}^{*}{\cal B}_{0}P_{j}f_{k}^{\sigma}+\frac{1}{2}P_{0}^{*}P_{j}^{*}{\cal B}_{0}f_{k}^{\sigma}=\frac{1}{2}P_{0}^{*}{\cal B}_{0}P_{j}\Pi^{\angle}P_{0}f_{k}^{\sigma}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{lastid}}}{{=}}\frac{1}{2}\Pi_{0}^{+}P_{j}f_{k}^{\sigma}\,.

(3.42)

Using again that
P_{0}^{*}{\cal B}_{0}P_{j}f_{k}^{\sigma}=\Pi_{0}^{+}P_{j}f_{k}^{\sigma}
we obtain (
3.41
).
∎

Lemma 3.10
.

For any
f\in\{f_{1}^{+},f_{1}^{-},f_{0}^{-}\}
and
j\in\mathbb{N}
we have
\Pi_{0}^{+}P_{0,j}f=\Pi_{0}^{+}P_{j,0}f=0
.

Proof.

We have that
\Pi_{0}^{+}P_{0,j}f=0
if and only if
(P_{0,j}f,f_{0}^{+})=0
.
By
[
10
, formula (4.8)]
we have that

P_{0,\epsilon}f_{0}^{-}=f_{0}^{-}
for any
\epsilon
and
we have the chain of identities

\big(P_{0,\epsilon}f,f_{0}^{+}\big)=-\big(\mathcal{J}P_{0,\epsilon}f,\mathcal{J}f_{0}^{+}\big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{propPU},\mathcal{J}f_{0}^{+}=-f_{0}^{-}}}{{=}}\big(P_{0,\epsilon}^{*}\mathcal{J}f,f_{0}^{-}\big)=\big(\mathcal{J}f,P_{0,\epsilon}f_{0}^{-}\big)=\big(\mathcal{J}f,f_{0}^{-}\big)=0

for any
f\in\{f_{1}^{+},f_{1}^{-},f_{0}^{-}\}
, deducing,
in particular, that
(P_{0,j}f,f_{0}^{+})=0
.
The proof that
\Pi_{0}^{+}P_{j,0}f=0
is obtained similarly, exploiting that

P_{\mu,0}f_{0}^{-}=f_{0}^{-}
as proved in
[
10
, Lemma A.5]
.
∎

In virtue of (
3.6
), (
3.11
) and (
3.15
) and in view of (
3.24
)-(
3.25
) we obtain

\mathfrak{B}_{i,j}^{[\mathtt{ev}]}=\begin{cases}\mathfrak{B}_{i,j}&\textup{if }j\textup{ is even}\,,\\
0&\textup{if }j\textup{ is odd}\,,\end{cases}\qquad\mathfrak{B}_{i,j}^{[\mathtt{odd}]}=\begin{cases}0&\textup{if }j\textup{ is even}\,,\\
\mathfrak{B}_{i,j}&\textup{if }j\textup{ is odd}\,.\end{cases}

(3.43)

Proof of Proposition
3.1

Proposition
3.1
is a direct consequence of the next proposition.

.

\displaystyle\widetilde{\phi}_{22}:=\big(\textup{$\mathfrak{B}_{2,2}$}f_{0}^{-},f_{1}^{-}\big)=0\,,\qquad\mathrm{i}\,\widetilde{\psi}_{12}:=\big(\textup{$\mathfrak{B}_{2,1}$}f_{0}^{-},f_{1}^{+}\big)=0\,,

whereas the coefficients

The rest of the section is devoted to the proof of Proposition

.

Lemma 3.12
.

The coefficients
\widetilde{\eta}_{11},\widetilde{\eta}_{22},\widetilde{\gamma}_{22},\widetilde{\phi}_{12},\widetilde{\phi}_{21},\widetilde{\phi}_{22}
in (
3.45a
) vanish.

Proof.

The first six
coefficients
in (
3.45a
) are (use also the self-adjointness of the jets of
\mathfrak{B}_{\mu,\epsilon}
)

\big(\textup{$\mathfrak{B}_{0,3}^{[\mathtt{ev}]}$}f_{1}^{+},f_{1}^{+}\big)\,,\ \big(\textup{$\mathfrak{B}_{2,1}^{[\mathtt{ev}]}$}f_{1}^{-},f_{1}^{-}\big)\,,\ \big(\textup{$\mathfrak{B}_{2,1}^{[\mathtt{ev}]}$}f_{0}^{-},f_{0}^{-}\big)\,,\ \big(\textup{$\mathfrak{B}_{1,2}^{[\mathtt{odd}]}$}f_{0}^{-},f_{1}^{+}\big)\,,\ \big(f_{0}^{+},\textup{$\mathfrak{B}_{1,2}^{[\mathtt{odd}]}$}f_{1}^{-}\big)\,,\ \big(\textup{$\mathfrak{B}_{2,2}^{[\mathtt{odd}]}$}f_{0}^{-},f_{1}^{-}\big)\,,

which are zero because,
by (
3.43
), the operators

\mathfrak{B}_{0,3}^{[\mathtt{ev}]}\mathfrak{B}_{1,2}^{[\mathtt{odd}]}=\mathfrak{B}_{2,1}^{[\mathtt{ev}]}=\mathfrak{B}_{2,2}^{[\mathtt{odd}]}=0
.
∎

For the computation of the other coefficients we use the following lemma.

Lemma 3.13
.

We have

\displaystyle{\cal B}_{0,1}f_{1}^{+}=\begin{bmatrix}\frac{1}{2}(a_{1}^{[1]}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{\frac{1}{2}}-p_{1}^{[1]}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-\frac{1}{2}})\cos(2x)\\

\mathfrak{h}^{[\kappa_{1},\dots,\kappa_{\ell}]}(x)
denotes a function supported on Fourier modes
\kappa_{1},\dots,\kappa_{\ell}\in{\mathbb{N}_{0}}
.

{\cal B}_{0,1}f_{0}^{-}=0
we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{i}\,\phi_{12}

\displaystyle=\big({\cal B}_{1,3}f_{0}^{-},f_{1}^{+}\big)+\underbrace{\big({\cal B}_{0,3}P_{1,0}f_{0}^{-},f_{1}^{+}\big)}_{=0}+\underbrace{\big({\cal B}_{1,2}P_{0,1}f_{0}^{-},f_{1}^{+}\big)}_{=0}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\big({\cal B}_{0,2}P_{1,1}f_{0}^{-},f_{1}^{+}\big)}_{-\frac{\mathrm{i}\,}{2}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-3/2}\big(f_{-1}^{+},{\cal B}_{0,2}f_{1}^{+}\big)}+\underbrace{\big({\cal B}_{1,1}P_{0,2}f_{0}^{-},f_{1}^{+}\big)}_{=0}+\underbrace{\big({\cal B}_{1,0}P_{0,3}f_{0}^{-},f_{1}^{+}\big)}_{=0}+\underbrace{\big({\cal B}_{0,1}P_{1,2}f_{0}^{-},f_{1}^{+}\big)}_{\mathrm{i}\,\big({\scriptsize\begin{bmatrix}\mathfrak{a}_{1,2}\cos(2x)\\
\mathfrak{b}_{1,2}\sin(2x)\end{bmatrix}},{\cal B}_{0,1}f_{1}^{+}\big)}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{1}{2}\big(\Pi_{0}^{+}P_{1,3}f_{0}^{-},f_{1}^{+}\big)}_{=0}-\frac{1}{2}\underbrace{\big({\cal B}_{0,2}P_{0}P_{1,1}f_{0}^{-},f_{1}^{+}\big)}_{=0}-\frac{1}{2}\underbrace{\big({\cal B}_{0,2}f_{0}^{-},P_{0}P_{1,1}f_{1}^{+}\big)}_{=0}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\underbrace{\big({\cal B}_{1,1}P_{0}P_{0,2}f_{0}^{-},f_{1}^{+}\big)}_{=0}\underbrace{-\frac{1}{2}\big({\cal B}_{1,1}f_{0}^{-},P_{0}P_{0,2}f_{1}^{+}\big)}_{-\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{n}_{0,2}\big({\cal B}_{1,1}f_{0}^{-},f_{1}^{+}\big)}-\frac{1}{2}\underbrace{\big({\cal B}_{1,0}P_{0}P_{0,3}f_{0}^{-},f_{1}^{+}\big)}_{=0}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\underbrace{\big({\cal B}_{1,0}f_{0}^{-},P_{0}P_{0,3}f_{1}^{+}\big)}_{=0}-\frac{1}{2}\underbrace{\big({\cal B}_{0,1}P_{0}P_{1,2}f_{0}^{-},f_{1}^{+}\big)}_{=0}-\frac{1}{2}\underbrace{\big({\cal B}_{0,1}f_{0}^{-},P_{0}P_{1,2}f_{1}^{+}\big)}_{=0}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\underbrace{\big({\cal B}_{1,0}P_{0,1}P_{0}P_{0,2}f_{0}^{-},f_{1}^{+}\big)}_{=0}-\frac{1}{2}\underbrace{\big({\cal B}_{1,0}f_{0}^{-},P_{0,1}P_{0}P_{0,2}f_{1}^{+}\big)}_{=0}-\frac{1}{2}\underbrace{\big({\cal B}_{0,1}P_{1,0}P_{0}P_{0,2}f_{0}^{-},f_{1}^{+}\big)}_{=0}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\underbrace{\big({\cal B}_{0,1}f_{0}^{-},P_{1,0}P_{0}P_{0,2}f_{1}^{+}\big)}_{=0}-\frac{1}{2}\underbrace{\big({\cal B}_{0,1}P_{0,1}P_{0}P_{1,1}f_{0}^{-},f_{1}^{+}\big)}_{=0}-\frac{1}{2}\underbrace{\big({\cal B}_{0,1}f_{0}^{-},P_{0,1}P_{0}P_{1,1}f_{1}^{+}\big)}_{=0}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{1}{2}\big(\mathfrak{N}_{1,1}P_{0}P_{0,2}f_{0}^{-},f_{1}^{+}\big)}_{=0}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{2}\big(f_{0}^{-},\mathfrak{N}_{1,1}P_{0}P_{0,2}f_{1}^{+}\big)}_{=0}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{1}{2}\big(\mathfrak{N}_{0,2}P_{0}P_{1,1}f_{0}^{-},f_{1}^{+}\big)}_{=0}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{2}\big(f_{0}^{-},\mathfrak{N}_{0,2}P_{0}P_{1,1}f_{1}^{+}\big)}_{-\frac{\mathrm{i}\,}{2}\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}_{1,1}\big(f_{0}^{-},\mathfrak{N}_{0,2}f_{0}^{-}\big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{kN}}}{{=}}0}\,.

Hence by (
3.46
), (
1.15
), (
3.16
), we have

\displaystyle\phi_{12}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}p_{3}^{[1]}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{1/2}-\frac{1}{4}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(a_{2}^{[0]}+\frac{1}{2}a_{2}^{[2]})+\frac{1}{4}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-\frac{5}{2}}\mathtt{f}_{2}(1-{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}\mathfrak{a}_{1,2}(a_{1}^{[1]}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{\frac{1}{2}}-p_{1}^{[1]}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-\frac{1}{2}})-\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{b}_{1,2}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{\frac{1}{2}}p_{1}^{[1]}+\frac{1}{4}\mathfrak{n}_{0,2}p_{1}^{[1]}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\,,

which, in view of (
A.59
), (
3.22
) gives the term (
3.4h
).

4
Block-decoupling and proof of Theorem
1.3

In this section we prove Theorem
1.3
by block-decoupling
the
4\times 4
Hamiltonian matrix
\mathtt{L}_{\mu,\epsilon}=\mathtt{J}_{4}\mathtt{B}_{\mu,\epsilon}
in
(
2.8
) obtained
in Proposition
3.1
, expanding the computations of
[
10
]

at a higher degree of accuracy.

We first perform the singular symplectic and reversibility-preserving
change of coordinates in
[
10
, Lemma 5.1]
.

Lemma 4.1
.

(Singular symplectic rescaling)

The conjugation of the Hamiltonian and reversible matrix
\mathtt{L}_{\mu,\epsilon}=\mathtt{J}_{4}\mathtt{B}_{\mu,\epsilon}

in (
2.8
)
obtained in Proposition
3.1

through the symplectic and reversibility-preserving

4\times 4
-matrix

Y:=\begin{pmatrix}Q&0\\
0&Q\end{pmatrix}\quad\text{with}\quad Q:=\begin{pmatrix}\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}&0\\
0&\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\end{pmatrix}\,,\ \ \mu>0\,,

yields the Hamiltonian and reversible matrix

\displaystyle\mathtt{L}_{\mu,\epsilon}^{(1)}:=Y^{-1}\mathtt{L}_{\mu,\epsilon}Y=\mathtt{J}_{4}\mathtt{B}^{(1)}_{\mu,\epsilon}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathtt{J}_{2}E^{(1)}&\mathtt{J}_{2}F^{(1)}\\
\mathtt{J}_{2}[F^{(1)}]^{*}&\mathtt{J}_{2}G^{(1)}\end{pmatrix}

(4.1)

where
\mathtt{B}_{\mu,\epsilon}^{(1)}
is a self-adjoint and reversibility-preserving

4\times 4
matrix

\mathtt{B}_{\mu,\epsilon}^{(1)}=\begin{pmatrix}E^{(1)}&F^{(1)}\\
[F^{(1)}]^{*}&G^{(1)}\end{pmatrix},\quad E^{(1)}=[E^{(1)}]^{*}\,,\ G^{(1)}=[G^{(1)}]^{*}\,,

where the
2\times 2
reversibility-preserving matrices
E^{(1)}:=E^{(1)}(\mu,\epsilon)
,
G^{(1)}:=G^{(1)}(\mu,\epsilon)
and
F^{(1)}:=F^{(1)}(\mu,\epsilon)
extend analytically at
\mu=0
with
the expansion

\displaystyle E^{(1)}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathtt{e}_{11}\mu\epsilon^{2}(1+r_{1}^{\prime}(\epsilon^{3},\mu\epsilon))+\eta_{11}\mu\epsilon^{4}-\mathtt{e}_{22}\frac{\mu^{3}}{8}(1+r_{1}^{\prime\prime}(\epsilon,\mu))&\mathrm{i}\,\big(\frac{1}{2}\mathtt{e}_{12}\mu+\eta_{12}\mu\epsilon^{2}+r_{2}(\mu\epsilon^{3},\mu^{2}\epsilon,\mu^{3})\big)\\
-\mathrm{i}\,\big(\frac{1}{2}\mathtt{e}_{12}\mu+\eta_{12}\mu\epsilon^{2}+r_{2}(\mu\epsilon^{3},\mu^{2}\epsilon,\mu^{3})\big)&-\mathtt{e}_{22}\frac{\mu}{8}(1+r_{5}(\epsilon^{2},\mu))\end{pmatrix}

(4.2a)

\displaystyle G^{(1)}:=\begin{pmatrix}\mu+\gamma_{11}\mu\epsilon^{2}+r_{8}(\mu\epsilon^{3},{\mu^{2}\epsilon^{2}},\mu^{3}\epsilon)&-\mathrm{i}\,\gamma_{12}\mu\epsilon^{2}-\mathrm{i}\,r_{9}(\mu\epsilon^{3},\mu^{2}\epsilon)\\
\mathrm{i}\,\gamma_{12}\mu\epsilon^{2}+\mathrm{i}\,r_{9}(\mu\epsilon^{3},\mu^{2}\epsilon)&\tanh(\mathtt{h}\mu)+\gamma_{22}\mu\epsilon^{2}+r_{10}(\mu\epsilon^{3},\mu^{2}\epsilon)\end{pmatrix}

(4.2b)

\displaystyle F^{(1)}:=\begin{pmatrix}\mathtt{f}_{11}\mu\epsilon+\phi_{11}\mu\epsilon^{3}+r_{3}(\mu\epsilon^{4},\mu^{2}\epsilon^{2},\mu^{3}\epsilon)&\mathrm{i}\,\mu\epsilon{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}+\mathrm{i}\,\phi_{12}\mu\epsilon^{3}+\mathrm{i}\,r_{4}({\mu\epsilon^{4}},\mu^{2}\epsilon^{2},\mu^{3}\epsilon)\\
\mathrm{i}\,\phi_{21}\mu\epsilon+\mathrm{i}\,r_{6}(\mu\epsilon^{3},\mu^{2}\epsilon)&\phi_{22}\mu\epsilon+r_{7}(\mu\epsilon^{3},\mu^{2}\epsilon)\end{pmatrix}

(4.2c)

where the coefficients appearing in the entries are the same of (
3.3
).

Note that the matrix
\mathtt{L}_{\mu,\epsilon}^{(1)}
,
initially defined only for
\mu\neq 0
, extends analytically to the zero matrix at

\mu=0
.
For
\mu\neq 0
the spectrum of
\mathtt{L}_{\mu,\epsilon}^{(1)}
coincides with the spectrum of
\mathtt{L}_{\mu,\epsilon}
.

Non-perturbative step of block-decoupling.

The following lemma
computes the first order Taylor expansions (
4.4
)
of the matrix entries in (
4.3
)
and then the expansion (
4.8
) at a higher degree of accuracy
with respect to
[
10
, Lemma 5.4]
.

Lemma 4.2
.

(Step of block-decoupling)

There exists a
2\times 2
reversibility-preserving matrix
X
,
analytic in
(\mu,\epsilon)
, of the form

X:=\begin{pmatrix}x_{11}&\mathrm{i}\,x_{12}\\
\mathrm{i}\,x_{21}&x_{22}\end{pmatrix}\,,\qquad x_{ij}\in\mathbb{R}\,,\ i,j=1,2\,,

(4.3)

with

\displaystyle x_{11}

\displaystyle=x_{11}^{(1)}\epsilon+r(\epsilon^{3},\mu\epsilon)\,,\qquad x_{12}=x_{12}^{(1)}\epsilon+r(\epsilon^{3},\mu\epsilon)

(4.4a)

\displaystyle x_{21}

\displaystyle=x_{21}^{(1)}\epsilon+x_{21}^{(3)}\epsilon^{3}+r(\epsilon^{4},\mu\epsilon^{2},\mu^{2}\epsilon)\,,\qquad x_{22}=x_{22}^{(1)}\epsilon+x_{22}^{(3)}\epsilon^{3}+r(\epsilon^{4},\mu\epsilon^{2},\mu^{2}\epsilon)\,,

where

x_{21}^{(1)}:=-\tfrac{1}{2}\mathtt{D}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-1}\big(\mathtt{e}_{12}\mathtt{f}_{11}+2{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big)\,,\quad x_{22}^{(1)}:=\tfrac{1}{2}\mathtt{D}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-1}\big({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathtt{e}_{12}+2\mathtt{h}\mathtt{f}_{11}\big)\,,

(4.4b)

and

\displaystyle x_{11}^{(1)}:

\displaystyle=\mathtt{D}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-1}\big(\tfrac{1}{16}\mathtt{e}_{12}\mathtt{e}_{22}x_{21}^{(1)}-\tfrac{1}{2}\mathtt{e}_{12}\phi_{21}+\phi_{22}-\tfrac{1}{8}\mathtt{e}_{22}x_{22}^{(1)}\big)\,,

(4.4c)

\displaystyle x_{12}^{(1)}:

\displaystyle=\mathtt{D}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-1}\big(\tfrac{1}{8}\mathtt{h}\mathtt{e}_{22}x_{21}^{(1)}-\mathtt{h}\phi_{21}+\tfrac{1}{2}\mathtt{e}_{12}\phi_{22}-\tfrac{1}{16}\mathtt{e}_{12}\mathtt{e}_{22}x_{22}^{(1)}\big)\,,

\displaystyle x_{21}^{(3)}:

\displaystyle=\mathtt{D}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-1}\big(-\tfrac{1}{2}\mathtt{e}_{11}\mathtt{e}_{12}x_{11}^{(1)}+\tfrac{1}{2}(\gamma_{12}+\eta_{12})\mathtt{e}_{12}x_{21}^{(1)}+\tfrac{1}{2}\mathtt{e}_{12}\gamma_{11}x_{22}^{(1)}

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{2}\phi_{11}\mathtt{e}_{12}-\mathtt{e}_{11}x_{12}^{(1)}-\gamma_{22}x_{21}^{(1)}-(\gamma_{12}+\eta_{12})x_{22}^{(1)}-\phi_{12}\big)\,,

\displaystyle x_{22}^{(3)}:

\displaystyle={\mathtt{D}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-1}\big(\mathtt{h}\mathtt{e}_{11}x_{11}^{(1)}-\mathtt{h}(\gamma_{12}+\eta_{12})x_{21}^{(1)}-\mathtt{h}\gamma_{11}x_{22}^{(1)}+\mathtt{h}\phi_{11}}

\displaystyle+\tfrac{1}{2}\mathtt{e}_{11}\mathtt{e}_{12}x_{12}^{(1)}+\tfrac{1}{2}\mathtt{e}_{12}\gamma_{22}x_{21}^{(1)}+\tfrac{1}{2}\mathtt{e}_{12}(\gamma_{12}+\eta_{12})x_{22}^{(1)}+{\tfrac{1}{2}\mathtt{e}_{12}}\phi_{12}\big)\,,

with

\mathtt{e}_{12}
,
\mathtt{e}_{22}
,
\mathtt{e}_{11}
,

\phi_{21},\phi_{22},\gamma_{12},\eta_{12},\gamma_{11},\phi_{11},\gamma_{22},\phi_{12},\mathtt{f}_{11}
computed
in (
3.4
) and
(cfr.
[
10
, (5.7)]
)

\mathtt{D}_{\mathtt{h}}:=\mathtt{h}-\frac{1}{4}\mathtt{e}_{12}^{2}>0\,,\quad\forall\mathtt{h}>0\,,

(4.5)

such that the following holds true. By conjugating the Hamiltonian and reversible matrix

\mathtt{L}_{\mu,\epsilon}^{(1)}
, defined in (
4.1
), with the symplectic and reversibility-preserving
4\times 4
matrix

\exp\big(S^{(1)}\big)\,,\quad\text{ where }\qquad S^{(1)}:=\mathtt{J}_{4}\begin{pmatrix}0&\Sigma\\
\Sigma^{*}&0\end{pmatrix}\,,\qquad\Sigma:=\mathtt{J}_{2}X\,,

(4.6)

we get the Hamiltonian and reversible matrix

\mathtt{L}_{\mu,\epsilon}^{(2)}:=\exp\big(S^{(1)}\big)\mathtt{L}_{\mu,\epsilon}^{(1)}\exp\big(-S^{(1)}\big)=\mathtt{J}_{4}\mathtt{B}_{\mu,\epsilon}^{(2)}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathtt{J}_{2}E^{(2)}&\mathtt{J}_{2}F^{(2)}\\
\mathtt{J}_{2}[F^{(2)}]^{*}&\mathtt{J}_{2}G^{(2)}\end{pmatrix}\,,

(4.7)

where the reversibility-preserving
2\times 2
self-adjoint
matrix
E^{(2)}
has the form

\displaystyle E^{(2)}={\begin{pmatrix}\mathtt{e}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsc{WB}}}\mu\epsilon^{2}+\eta_{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsc{WB}}}\mu\epsilon^{4}+r_{1}^{\prime}(\mu\epsilon^{5},\mu^{2}\epsilon^{3})-\mathtt{e}_{22}\frac{\mu^{3}}{8}(1+r_{1}^{\prime\prime}(\epsilon,\mu))&\mathrm{i}\,\big(\frac{1}{2}\mathtt{e}_{12}\mu+r_{2}(\mu\epsilon^{2},\mu^{2}\epsilon,\mu^{3})\big)\\
-\mathrm{i}\,\big(\frac{1}{2}\mathtt{e}_{12}\mu+r_{2}(\mu\epsilon^{2},\mu^{2}\epsilon,\mu^{3})\big)&-\mathtt{e}_{22}\frac{\mu}{8}(1+r_{5}(\epsilon,\mu))\end{pmatrix}}\,,

(4.8)

where

\mathtt{e}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsc{WB}}}

is the Whitham-Benjamin function in (
1.3
) and

\displaystyle\eta_{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsc{WB}}}=\eta_{11}+{x_{21}^{(1)}\phi_{12}+x_{21}^{(3)}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}-x_{22}^{(1)}\phi_{11}-x_{22}^{(3)}\mathtt{f}_{11}+\frac{3}{2}(x_{21}^{(1)})^{2}x_{22}^{(1)}\phi_{22}+(x_{21}^{(1)})^{2}x_{12}^{(1)}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}}

(4.9)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad{-\frac{3}{2}x_{21}^{(1)}x_{12}^{(1)}x_{22}^{(1)}\mathtt{f}_{11}+\frac{3}{2}(x_{22}^{(1)})^{2}x_{21}^{(1)}\phi_{21}-\frac{3}{2}x_{22}^{(1)}x_{11}^{(1)}x_{21}^{(1)}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}+(x_{22}^{(1)})^{2}{x_{11}^{(1)}\mathtt{f}_{11}}}

with
x_{11}^{(1)}
,
x_{12}^{(1)}
,
x_{22}^{(1)}
,
x_{21}^{(1)}
,

x_{21}^{(3)}
,
x_{22}^{(3)}
in (
4.4
) and
the remaining coefficients in (
3.4
), whereas
the reversibility-preserving
2\times 2
self-adjoint
matrix
G^{(2)}
has the form

G^{(2)}=\begin{pmatrix}\mu+r_{8}(\mu\epsilon^{2},\mu^{3}\epsilon)&-\mathrm{i}\,r_{9}(\mu\epsilon^{2},\mu^{2}\epsilon)\\
\mathrm{i}\,r_{9}(\mu\epsilon^{2},\mu^{2}\epsilon)&\tanh(\mathtt{h}\mu)+r_{10}(\mu\epsilon)\end{pmatrix}\,,

(4.10)

and finally

F^{(2)}=\begin{pmatrix}r_{3}(\mu\epsilon^{3})&\mathrm{i}\,r_{4}(\mu\epsilon^{3})\\
\mathrm{i}\,r_{6}(\mu\epsilon^{3})&r_{7}(\mu\epsilon^{3})\end{pmatrix}\,.

(4.11)

The rest of the section is devoted to the proof of Lemma
4.2
.
In Lemma 5.4 of
[
10
]
we proved
the existence of a matrix
X
as in (
4.3
) such that we
obtain (
4.7
) with matrices
G^{(2)},F^{(2)}
as in
(
4.10
)-(
4.11
)
and a
2\times 2
-self adjoint and reversibility preserving
matrix
E^{(2)}
whose first entry has the form

[E^{(2)}]_{11}=\mathtt{e}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsc{WB}}}\mu\epsilon^{2}+r_{1}(\mu\epsilon^{3},\mu^{2}\epsilon^{2})
. The main result of Lemma

4.2
is
that the first entry
[E^{(2)}]_{11}

has the better expansion

[E^{(2)}]_{11}=\mathtt{e}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsc{WB}}}\mu\epsilon^{2}+r_{1}(\mu\epsilon^{3},\mu^{2}\epsilon^{2})=\mathtt{e}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsc{WB}}}\mu\epsilon^{2}+\eta_{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsc{WB}}}\mu\epsilon^{4}+r_{1}^{\prime}(\mu\epsilon^{5},\mu^{2}\epsilon^{3})\,

with
\eta_{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsc{WB}}}
computed in (
4.9
),
which is relevant to determine the stability/instablity of the Stokes wave at the
critical depth. Clearly we could compute explicitly also other Taylor coefficients
of the matrix entries of

E^{(2)},G^{(2)},F^{(2)}
, but it is not needed.

The coefficients

x_{21}^{(1)}
and
x_{22}^{(1)}
in (
4.4b
)
were already computed in
[
10
, Lemma 5.4]
.

We now expand in Lie series
the Hamiltonian and reversible matrix
\mathtt{L}_{\mu,\epsilon}^{(2)}=\exp(S)\mathtt{L}_{\mu,\epsilon}^{(1)}\exp(-S)

where for simplicity we set
S:=S^{(1)}
.
We split
\mathtt{L}_{\mu,\epsilon}^{(1)}

into its
2\times 2
-diagonal and off-diagonal Hamiltonian and reversible matrices

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\mathtt{L}_{\mu,\epsilon}^{(1)}=D^{(1)}+R^{(1)}\,,

(4.12)

\displaystyle D^{(1)}:=\begin{pmatrix}D_{1}&0\\
0&D_{0}\end{pmatrix}:=\begin{pmatrix}\mathtt{J}_{2}E^{(1)}&0\\
0&\mathtt{J}_{2}G^{(1)}\end{pmatrix},\quad R^{(1)}:=\begin{pmatrix}0&\mathtt{J}_{2}F^{(1)}\\
\mathtt{J}_{2}[F^{(1)}]^{*}&0\end{pmatrix},

and we perform the Lie expansion

\displaystyle\mathtt{L}_{\mu,\epsilon}^{(2)}=\exp(S)\mathtt{L}_{\mu,\epsilon}^{(1)}\exp(-S)=D^{(1)}+[S,D^{(1)}]+\frac{1}{2}\big[S,[S,D^{(1)}]\big]+R^{(1)}+[S,R^{(1)}]

(4.13)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}(1-\tau)^{2}\exp(\tau S)\text{ad}_{S}^{3}(D^{(1)})\exp(-\tau S)\,\mathrm{d}\tau+\int_{0}^{1}(1-\tau)\,\exp(\tau S)\,\text{ad}_{S}^{2}(R^{(1)})\,\exp(-\tau S)\,\mathrm{d}\tau

where
\text{ad}_{A}(B):=[A,B]:=AB-BA
denotes the commutator
between the linear operators
A,B
.

We look for a
4\times 4
matrix
S
as in (
4.6
)
that solves
the homological equation

R^{(1)}+\left[S\,,\,D^{(1)}\right]=0
,
which, recalling (
4.12
), reads

\begin{pmatrix}0&\mathtt{J}_{2}F^{(1)}+\mathtt{J}_{2}\Sigma D_{0}-D_{1}\mathtt{J}_{2}\Sigma\\
\mathtt{J}_{2}{[F^{(1)}]}^{*}+\mathtt{J}_{2}\Sigma^{*}D_{1}-D_{0}\mathtt{J}_{2}\Sigma^{*}&0\end{pmatrix}=0\,.

(4.14)

Writing
\Sigma=\mathtt{J}_{2}X
, namely
X=-\mathtt{J}_{2}\Sigma
,
the equation (
4.14
) amounts to solve the “Sylvester” equation

D_{1}X-XD_{0}=-\mathtt{J}_{2}F^{(1)}\,.

(4.15)

We write the matrices
E^{(1)},F^{(1)},G^{(1)}
in (
4.1
)
as

E^{(1)}=\begin{pmatrix}E_{11}^{(1)}&\mathrm{i}\,E_{12}^{(1)}\\
-\mathrm{i}\,E_{12}^{(1)}&E_{22}^{(1)}\end{pmatrix}\,,\quad F^{(1)}=\begin{pmatrix}F_{11}^{(1)}&\mathrm{i}\,F_{12}^{(1)}\\
\mathrm{i}\,F_{21}^{(1)}&F_{22}^{(1)}\end{pmatrix}\,,\quad G^{(1)}=\begin{pmatrix}G_{11}^{(1)}&\mathrm{i}\,G_{12}^{(1)}\\
-\mathrm{i}\,G_{12}^{(1)}&G_{22}^{(1)}\end{pmatrix}

(4.16)

where the real numbers

E_{ij}^{(1)},F_{ij}^{(1)},G_{ij}^{(1)}
,
i,j=1,2
, have the expansion
in (
4.2a
), (
4.2b
), (
4.2c
).
Thus, by (
4.12
), (
4.3
) and (
4.16
),
the equation (
4.15
) amounts to solve the

4\times 4
real linear system

\displaystyle\underbrace{\begin{pmatrix}G_{12}^{(1)}-E_{12}^{(1)}&G_{11}^{(1)}&E_{22}^{(1)}&0\\
G_{22}^{(1)}&G_{12}^{(1)}-E_{12}^{(1)}&0&-E_{22}^{(1)}\\
E_{11}^{(1)}&0&G_{12}^{(1)}-E_{12}^{(1)}&-G_{11}^{(1)}\\
0&-E_{11}^{(1)}&-G_{22}^{(1)}&G_{12}^{(1)}-E_{12}^{(1)}\end{pmatrix}}_{=:{\cal A}}\underbrace{\begin{pmatrix}x_{11}\\
x_{12}\\
x_{21}\\
x_{22}\end{pmatrix}}_{=:\vec{x}}=\underbrace{\begin{pmatrix}-F_{21}^{(1)}\\
F_{22}^{(1)}\\
-F_{11}^{(1)}\\
F_{12}^{(1)}\end{pmatrix}}_{=:\vec{f}}.

(4.17)

By
[
10
]
system (
4.17
) admits a unique solution. We now prove
that it has the form (
4.4
).

Lemma 4.3
.

The vector
\vec{x}=(x_{11},x_{12},x_{21},x_{22})
with entries in (
4.4
)
solves (
4.17
).

Proof.

Since
\tanh(\mathtt{h}\mu)=\mathtt{h}\mu+r(\mu^{3})
, we have

\displaystyle G_{12}^{(1)}-E_{12}^{(1)}=-\mathtt{e}_{12}\frac{\mu}{2}-(\gamma_{12}+\eta_{12})\mu\epsilon^{2}+r(\mu\epsilon^{3},\mu^{2}\epsilon,\mu^{3})\,,

(4.18)

\displaystyle G_{11}^{(1)}=\mu+\gamma_{11}\mu\epsilon^{2}+r_{8}(\mu\epsilon^{3},{\mu^{2}\epsilon^{2}},\mu^{3}\epsilon)\,,\ \ E_{22}^{(1)}=-\mathtt{e}_{22}\frac{\mu}{8}(1+r_{5}(\epsilon^{2},\mu))\,,

\displaystyle G_{22}^{(1)}=\mu\mathtt{h}+\gamma_{22}\mu\epsilon^{2}+r(\mu\epsilon^{3},\mu^{2}\epsilon,\mu^{3})\,,\ \ E_{11}^{(1)}=\mathtt{e}_{11}\mu\epsilon^{2}+r(\mu\epsilon^{4},\mu^{2}\epsilon^{3},\mu^{3})\,,

with coefficients
\mathtt{e}_{12}
,
\gamma_{12}
,
\eta_{12}
,
\gamma_{11}
,
\mathtt{e}_{22}
,
\gamma_{22}
and
\mathtt{e}_{11}
computed in (
3.4
). We exploit that the
terms
x_{21}^{(1)}
and
x_{22}^{(2)}
have been already computed in

[
10
, Lemma 5.4]
, in order to get
x_{11}^{(1)}
and
x_{12}^{(2)}
in (
4.4
) as solutions of the system

\begin{pmatrix}-\tfrac{1}{2}\mathtt{e}_{12}&1\\
\mathtt{h}&-\tfrac{1}{2}\mathtt{e}_{12}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}x_{11}^{(1)}\\
x_{12}^{(1)}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\tfrac{1}{8}\mathtt{e}_{22}x_{21}^{(1)}-\phi_{21}\\
\phi_{22}-\tfrac{1}{8}\mathtt{e}_{22}x_{22}^{(1)}\end{pmatrix}\,,\quad\det\begin{pmatrix}-\tfrac{1}{2}\mathtt{e}_{12}&1\\
\mathtt{h}&-\tfrac{1}{2}\mathtt{e}_{12}\end{pmatrix}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{defDh}}}{{=}}-\mathtt{D}_{\mathtt{h}}<0\,,

(4.19)

given, using also (
4.2c
),
by the first two lines in (
4.17
) at order
\mu\epsilon
.
Similarly,
x_{21}^{(3)}
and
x_{22}^{(3)}
in (
4.4
) solve the system

\begin{pmatrix}-\tfrac{1}{2}\mathtt{e}_{12}&-1\\
-\mathtt{h}&-\tfrac{1}{2}\mathtt{e}_{12}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}x_{21}^{(3)}\\
x_{22}^{(3)}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}-\mathtt{e}_{11}x_{11}^{(1)}+(\gamma_{12}+\eta_{12})x_{21}^{(1)}+\gamma_{11}x_{22}^{(1)}-\phi_{11}\\
\mathtt{e}_{11}x_{12}^{(1)}+\gamma_{22}x_{21}^{(1)}+(\gamma_{12}+\eta_{12})x_{22}^{(1)}+\phi_{12}\end{pmatrix}\,,

(4.20)

which comes, also by (
4.2c
), from
the last two lines of (
4.17
) at order
\mu\epsilon^{3}
.
The solutions of (
4.19
)-(
4.20
) are given in (
4.4c
).
∎

We now prove the expansion (
4.8
).
Since the matrix
S
solves the homological equation
\left[S\,,\,D^{(1)}\right]+R^{(1)}=0
, identity (
4.13
) simplifies to

\mathtt{L}_{\mu,\epsilon}^{(2)}=D^{(1)}+\frac{1}{2}\big[S,R^{(1)}\big]+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}(1-\tau^{2})\,\exp(\tau S)\,\text{ad}_{S}^{2}(R^{(1)})\,\exp(-\tau S)\mathrm{d}\tau\,.

(4.21)

By plugging the Lie expansion

\displaystyle\exp(\tau S)\,\text{ad}_{S}^{2}(R^{(1)})\,\exp(-\tau S)

\displaystyle=\text{ad}_{S}^{2}(R^{(1)})+\tau\text{ad}_{S}^{3}(R^{(1)})+\tau^{2}\int_{0}^{1}(1-\tau^{\prime})\exp(\tau^{\prime}\tau S)\,\text{ad}_{S}^{4}(R^{(1)})\,\exp(-\tau^{\prime}\tau S)\,\mathrm{d}\tau^{\prime}

into (
4.21
) we get

\displaystyle\mathtt{L}_{\mu,\epsilon}^{(2)}=D^{(1)}+\frac{1}{2}\big[S,R^{(1)}\big]+\frac{1}{3}\text{ad}_{S}^{2}(R^{(1)})+\frac{1}{8}\text{ad}_{S}^{3}(R^{(1)})

(4.22a)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}(1-\tau^{2})\tau^{2}\int_{0}^{1}(1-\tau^{\prime})\exp(\tau\tau^{\prime}S)\text{ad}_{S}^{4}(R^{(1)})\exp(-\tau\tau^{\prime}S)\mathrm{d}\tau^{\prime}\mathrm{d}\tau\,.

(4.22b)

Next we compute the commutators in the expansion (
4.22a
).

Lemma 4.4
.

One has

\frac{1}{2}\big[S,R^{(1)}\big]=\begin{pmatrix}\mathtt{J}_{2}\tilde{E}_{1}&0\\
0&\mathtt{J}_{2}\tilde{G}_{1}\end{pmatrix}

(4.23)

where
\tilde{E}_{1}
,
\tilde{G}_{1}

are self-adjoint and reversibility-preserving matrices of the form

\displaystyle\tilde{E}_{1}=\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{\mathtt{e}}_{11}\mu\epsilon^{2}+\tilde{\eta}_{11}^{(a)}\mu\epsilon^{4}+\tilde{r}_{1}(\mu\epsilon^{5},\mu^{2}\epsilon^{3},\mu^{3}\epsilon^{2})&\mathrm{i}\,\big(\tilde{\mathtt{e}}_{12}\mu\epsilon^{2}+\tilde{r}_{2}(\mu\epsilon^{4},\mu^{2}\epsilon^{2})\big)\\
-\mathrm{i}\,\big(\tilde{\mathtt{e}}_{12}\mu\epsilon^{2}+\tilde{r}_{2}(\mu\epsilon^{4},\mu^{2}\epsilon^{2})\big)&\tilde{r}_{5}(\mu\epsilon^{2})\end{pmatrix}\,,

(4.24)

\displaystyle\tilde{G}_{1}=\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{\mathtt{g}}_{11}\mu\epsilon^{2}+\tilde{r}_{8}(\mu\epsilon^{4},\mu^{2}\epsilon^{2})&\mathrm{i}\,\big(\tilde{\mathtt{g}}_{12}\mu\epsilon^{2}+\tilde{r}_{9}(\mu\epsilon^{4},\mu^{2}\epsilon^{2})\big)\\
-\mathrm{i}\,\big(\tilde{\mathtt{g}}_{12}\mu\epsilon^{2}+\tilde{r}_{9}(\mu\epsilon^{4},\mu^{2}\epsilon^{2})\big)&\tilde{\mathtt{g}}_{22}\mu\epsilon^{2}+\tilde{r}_{10}(\mu\epsilon^{4},\mu^{2}\epsilon^{2})\end{pmatrix}\,,

where

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathtt{e}}_{11}:=x_{21}^{(1)}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}-x_{22}^{(1)}\mathtt{f}_{11}\,,\quad\tilde{\eta}_{11}^{(a)}:=x_{21}^{(1)}\phi_{12}+x_{21}^{(3)}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}-x_{22}^{(1)}\phi_{11}-x_{22}^{(3)}\mathtt{f}_{11}\,,\quad

(4.25)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathtt{e}}_{12}:=-\tilde{\mathtt{g}}_{12}:={\frac{1}{2}\big(x_{21}^{(1)}\phi_{22}+x_{22}^{(1)}\phi_{21}-x_{11}^{(1)}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}-x_{12}^{(1)}\mathtt{f}_{11}\big)}\,,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathtt{g}}_{11}:=x_{11}^{(1)}\mathtt{f}_{11}+x_{21}^{(1)}\phi_{21}\,,\quad\tilde{\mathtt{g}}_{22}:=x_{22}^{(1)}\phi_{22}+x_{12}^{(1)}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\,.

Proof.

By (
4.6
), (
4.12
), and since
\Sigma=\mathtt{J}_{2}X
, we have

\frac{1}{2}\left[S\,,\,R^{(1)}\right]=\begin{pmatrix}\mathtt{J}_{2}\tilde{E}_{1}&0\\
0&\mathtt{J}_{2}\tilde{G}_{1}\end{pmatrix},\quad\tilde{E}_{1}:=\textup{{Sym}}\big[\mathtt{J}_{2}X\mathtt{J}_{2}[F^{(1)}]^{*}\big]\,,\quad\tilde{G}_{1}:=\textup{{Sym}}\big[X^{*}F^{(1)}\big]\,,

(4.26)

where
\textup{{Sym}}[A]:=\frac{1}{2}(A+A^{*})
, see
[
10
, (5.28)-(5.29)]
.
By (
4.3
), (
4.16
), setting
F=F^{(1)}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathtt{J}_{2}X\mathtt{J}_{2}F^{*}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}x_{21}F_{12}-x_{22}F_{11}&\mathrm{i}\,(x_{21}F_{22}+x_{22}F_{21})\\
\mathrm{i}\,(x_{11}F_{12}+x_{12}F_{11})&-x_{11}F_{22}+x_{12}F_{21}\end{pmatrix}\,,

(4.27)

\displaystyle X^{*}F

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}x_{11}F_{11}+x_{21}F_{21}&\mathrm{i}\,(x_{11}F_{12}-x_{21}F_{22})\\
\mathrm{i}\,(x_{22}F_{21}-x_{12}F_{11})&x_{22}F_{22}+x_{12}F_{12}\end{pmatrix}\,,

(4.28)

and the expansions in (
4.24
) with the coefficients given in (
4.25
) follow by
(
4.27
), (
4.28
), (
4.4
) and (
4.2c
).
∎

Lemma 4.5
.

One has

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\mathrm{ad}_{S}^{2}(R^{(1)})=\begin{pmatrix}0&\mathtt{J}_{2}\tilde{F}\\
\mathtt{J}_{2}\tilde{F}^{*}&0\end{pmatrix},

(4.29)

where
\tilde{F}
is
a reversibility-preserving matrix of the form

\tilde{F}=\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{\mathtt{f}}_{11}\mu\epsilon^{3}+\tilde{r}_{3}(\mu\epsilon^{5},\mu^{2}\epsilon^{3})&\mathrm{i}\,\tilde{\mathtt{f}}_{12}\mu\epsilon^{3}+\mathrm{i}\,\tilde{r}_{4}(\mu\epsilon^{5},\mu^{2}\epsilon^{3})\\
\mathrm{i}\,\tilde{r}_{6}(\mu\epsilon^{3})&\tilde{r}_{7}(\mu\epsilon^{3})\end{pmatrix}\,,

(4.30)

with

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathtt{f}}_{11}

\displaystyle:={\frac{4}{3}x_{21}^{(1)}x_{11}^{(1)}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}-\frac{4}{3}x_{22}^{(1)}x_{11}^{(1)}\mathtt{f}_{11}-\frac{4}{3}x_{22}^{(1)}x_{21}^{(1)}\phi_{21}+\frac{2}{3}x_{21}^{(1)}x_{12}^{(1)}\mathtt{f}_{11}-\frac{2}{3}(x_{21}^{(1)})^{2}\phi_{22}}\,,

(4.31)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathtt{f}}_{12}

\displaystyle:={\frac{4}{3}x_{21}^{(1)}x_{22}^{(1)}\phi_{22}+\frac{4}{3}x_{12}^{(1)}x_{21}^{(1)}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}-\frac{4}{3}x_{12}^{(1)}x_{22}^{(1)}\mathtt{f}_{11}+\frac{2}{3}(x_{22}^{(1)})^{2}\phi_{21}-\frac{2}{3}x_{11}^{(1)}x_{22}^{(1)}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}}\,.

Proof.

Using the form of
S
in (
4.6
) and
[S,R^{(1)}]
in (
4.23
) we deduce (
4.29
) with

\tilde{F}:=\frac{2}{3}\big(\mathtt{J}_{2}X\mathtt{J}_{2}\tilde{G}_{1}+\tilde{E}_{1}X\big)

(4.32)

where
\tilde{E}_{1}
and
\tilde{G}_{1}
are the matrices in (
4.24
).
Writing
\tilde{E}_{1}={\footnotesize\begin{pmatrix}{[\tilde{E}_{1}]}_{11}&\mathrm{i}\,{[\tilde{E}_{1}]}_{12}\\
-\mathrm{i}\,{[\tilde{E}_{1}]}_{12}&{[\tilde{E}_{1}]}_{22}\end{pmatrix}}
,

\tilde{G}_{1}={\footnotesize\begin{pmatrix}{[\tilde{G}_{1}]}_{11}&\mathrm{i}\,{[\tilde{G}_{1}]}_{12}\\
-\mathrm{i}\,{[\tilde{G}_{1}]}_{12}&{[\tilde{G}_{1}]}_{22}\end{pmatrix}}

we have, in view of (
4.3
),

\displaystyle\mathtt{J}_{2}X\mathtt{J}_{2}\tilde{G}_{1}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}x_{21}{[\tilde{G}_{1}]}_{12}-x_{22}{[\tilde{G}_{1}]}_{11}&\mathrm{i}\,\big(x_{21}{[\tilde{G}_{1}]}_{22}-x_{22}{[\tilde{G}_{1}]}_{12}\big)\\
\mathrm{i}\,\big(x_{11}{[\tilde{G}_{1}]}_{12}+x_{12}{[\tilde{G}_{1}]}_{11}\big)&-x_{11}{[\tilde{G}_{1}]}_{22}-x_{12}{[\tilde{G}_{1}]}_{12}\end{pmatrix}\,,

(4.33)

\displaystyle\tilde{E}_{1}X

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}x_{11}{[\tilde{E}_{1}]}_{11}-x_{21}{[\tilde{E}_{1}]}_{12}&\mathrm{i}\,\big(x_{12}{[\tilde{E}_{1}]}_{11}+x_{22}{[\tilde{E}_{1}]}_{12}\big)\\
\mathrm{i}\,\big(x_{21}{[\tilde{E}_{1}]}_{22}-x_{11}{[\tilde{E}_{1}]}_{12}\big)&x_{12}{[\tilde{E}_{1}]}_{12}+x_{22}{[\tilde{E}_{1}]}_{22}\end{pmatrix}\,.

By (
4.32
), (
4.33
), (
4.4
) and (
4.24
) we deduce that the matrix
\tilde{F}
has the expansion (

)
with

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathtt{f}}_{11}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{3}\big(x_{21}^{(1)}\tilde{\mathtt{g}}_{12}-x_{22}^{(1)}\tilde{\mathtt{g}}_{11}+x_{11}^{(1)}\tilde{\mathtt{e}}_{11}-x_{21}^{(1)}\tilde{\mathtt{e}}_{12}\big)\,,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathtt{f}}_{12}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{3}\big(x_{21}^{(1)}\tilde{\mathtt{g}}_{22}-x_{22}^{(1)}\tilde{\mathtt{g}}_{12}+x_{12}^{(1)}\tilde{\mathtt{e}}_{11}+x_{22}^{(1)}\tilde{\mathtt{e}}_{12}\big)\,,

which, by (
4.25
), gives (
4.31
).
∎

Lemma 4.6
.

One has

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\mathrm{ad}_{S}^{3}(R^{(1)})=\begin{pmatrix}\mathtt{J}_{2}\tilde{E}_{3}&0\\
0&\mathtt{J}_{2}\tilde{G}_{3}\end{pmatrix},

(4.34)

where
the self-adjoint and reversibility-preserving matrices

\tilde{E}_{3},\tilde{G}_{3}
in (

) have entries of size
\mathcal{O}(\mu\epsilon^{4})
. In particular the first entry of the matrix

has the expansion

{[\tilde{E}_{3}]}_{11}=\tilde{\eta}_{11}^{(b)}\mu\epsilon^{4}+r(\mu\epsilon^{5},\mu^{2}\epsilon^{4})

(4.35)

with

\displaystyle\tilde{\eta}_{11}^{(b)}

\displaystyle:={\frac{3}{2}(x_{21}^{(1)})^{2}x_{22}^{(1)}\phi_{22}+(x_{21}^{(1)})^{2}x_{12}^{(1)}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}-\frac{3}{2}x_{21}^{(1)}x_{12}^{(1)}x_{22}^{(1)}\mathtt{f}_{11}}

(4.36)

\displaystyle\ \quad{+\frac{3}{2}(x_{22}^{(1)})^{2}x_{21}^{(1)}\phi_{21}-\frac{3}{2}x_{22}^{(1)}x_{11}^{(1)}x_{21}^{(1)}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}+(x_{22}^{(1)})^{2}{x_{11}^{(1)}\mathtt{f}_{11}}}\,.

Proof.

Since
\frac{1}{8}\mathrm{ad}_{S}^{3}(R^{(1)})=\frac{3}{8}[S,\frac{1}{3}\mathrm{ad}_{S}^{2}(R^{(1)})]

and using (
4.29
), the identity
(
4.34
) holds with

\tilde{E}_{3}:=\frac{3}{4}\textup{{Sym}}\big[\mathtt{J}_{2}X\mathtt{J}_{2}[\tilde{F}]^{*}\big]\,,\qquad\tilde{G}_{3}:=\frac{3}{4}\textup{{Sym}}\big[X^{*}\tilde{F}\big]\,.

(4.37)

Since, by (
4.4
) the matrix
X
in (
4.3
)
has entries of size
\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)
and the matrix
\tilde{F}

in (
4.30
) has entries of size
\mathcal{O}(\mu\epsilon^{3})
we
deduce that the matrices
\tilde{E}_{3}
,
\tilde{G}_{3}
in (
4.37
) have entries of
size
\mathcal{O}(\mu\epsilon^{4})
.
By (
4.37
) and denoting
\tilde{F}={\footnotesize\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{F}_{11}&\mathrm{i}\,\tilde{F}_{12}\\
\mathrm{i}\,\tilde{F}_{21}&\tilde{F}_{22}\end{pmatrix}}
,
we deduce, similarly to (

),
that

which, by (

), gives (

) with

which by (
4.31
) gives (
4.36
).
∎

Finally we show that the term in (
4.22b
) is small.

Lemma 4.7
.

The
4\times 4
Hamiltonian and reversible matrix
\footnotesize\begin{pmatrix}\mathtt{J}_{2}\widehat{E}&\mathtt{J}_{2}\widehat{F}\\
\mathtt{J}_{2}[\widehat{F}]^{*}&\mathtt{J}_{2}\widehat{G}\end{pmatrix}
given by (
4.22b
)
has the
2\times 2

self-adjoint and reversibility-preserving blocks
\widehat{E}
,
\widehat{G}

and the
2\times 2
reversibility-preserving block
\widehat{F}
all with entries of size
\mathcal{O}(\mu\epsilon^{5})
.

Proof.

By the Hamiltonian and reversibility properties of
S
and
R^{(1)}
the matrix
\textup{ad}_{S}^{4}(R^{(1)})
is Hamiltonian and reversible and the same holds, for any
\tau,\tau^{\prime}\in[0,1]
, for

\exp(\tau\tau^{\prime}S)\,\textup{ad}_{S}^{4}(R^{(1)})\,\exp(-\tau\tau^{\prime}S)=[S,\textup{ad}_{S}^{3}(R^{(1)})](1+\mathcal{O}(\mu,\epsilon))\,.

(4.38)

The claimed estimate on the entries of the matrix given by (
4.22b
) follows by (
4.38
) and
because

S
in (
4.6
) has entries of size
\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)
and
\textup{ad}_{S}^{3}(R^{(1)})
in (
4.34
) has entries of size
\mathcal{O}(\mu\epsilon^{4})
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
4.2
.

It follows by (
4.22
), (
4.12
) and Lemmata
4.4
,
4.5
,
4.6
and

4.7
.
The matrix

has the expansion in (

), with

\mathtt{e}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsc{WB}}}:=\mathtt{e}_{11}+\tilde{\mathtt{e}}_{11}=\mathtt{e}_{11}-\mathtt{D}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-1}\big({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-1}+\mathtt{h}\mathtt{f}_{11}^{2}+\mathtt{e}_{12}\mathtt{f}_{11}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big)\,,\qquad\eta_{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsc{WB}}}:=\eta_{11}+\tilde{\eta}_{11}^{(a)}+\tilde{\eta}_{11}^{(b)}\,,

as in (

Furthermore

∎

Complete block-decoupling and proof of the main result.

Finally Theorem
1.3
is proved as in
[
10
]

by block-diagonalizing the
4\times 4
Hamiltonian and reversible
matrix
\mathtt{L}_{\mu,\epsilon}^{(2)}
in (

),

\displaystyle\mathtt{L}_{\mu,\epsilon}^{(2)}=D^{(2)}+R^{(2)}\,,\quad D^{(2)}:=\begin{pmatrix}\mathtt{J}_{2}E^{(2)}&0\\
0&\mathtt{J}_{2}G^{(2)}\end{pmatrix},\quad R^{(2)}:=\begin{pmatrix}0&\mathtt{J}_{2}F^{(2)}\\
\mathtt{J}_{2}[F^{(2)}]^{*}&0\end{pmatrix}.

(4.39)

The next lemma is
[
10
, Lemma 5.9]
.

Lemma 4.8
.

There exist a
4\times 4
reversibility-preserving Hamiltonian matrix

of the form (

), analytic in
(\mu,\epsilon)
, of size

\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{3})
, and a
4\times 4

, analytic in
(\mu,\epsilon)
, of size
{\mathcal{O}(\mu\epsilon^{6})}
such that

\exp(S^{(2)})(D^{(2)}+R^{(2)})\exp(-S^{(2)})=D^{(2)}+P\,.

(4.40)

By (

), (
4.8
)-(

) and the fact that

has size
{\mathcal{O}(\mu\epsilon^{6})}
we deduce Theorem
1.3
:
there exists a symplectic and reversibility-preserving linear map that conjugates
the matrix
\mathrm{i}\,{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\mu+\mathtt{L}_{\mu,\epsilon}
(which represents
{\cal L}_{\mu,\epsilon}
)
with
\mathtt{L}_{\mu,\epsilon}
in (

)
into the Hamiltonian and reversible matrix (

) with

in (

) and

in (

The function

expands as in (

).

Appendix A
Fourth-order expansion of the Stokes waves

In this Appendix we compute the Taylor coefficients (

)
of the fourth order expansions (

) of the Stokes waves.
We also compute

) of the

-periodic functions

and

and

) of the constant

in (

).

A.1
Expansion of Stokes waves

Proposition A.1
.

(Expansion of Stokes waves)
The Stokes waves

\eta_{\epsilon}(x)
,
\psi_{\epsilon}(x)
and the speed
c_{\epsilon}
in Theorem
1.2
have the expansions
(
1.9
) with coefficients

\displaystyle\eta_{2}^{[0]}:=\frac{{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}-1}{4{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}}\,,\qquad\eta_{2}^{[2]}:=\frac{3-{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}}{4{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{6}}\,,\qquad\psi_{2}^{[2]}:=\frac{3+{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{8}}{8{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{7}}\,,\qquad

(A.1a)

\displaystyle c_{2}:=\frac{9-10{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}+9{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{8}}{16{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{7}}+{\frac{(1-{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4})}{2{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}}}\eta_{2}^{[0]}=\frac{-2{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{12}+13{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{8}-12{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}+9}{16{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{7}}\,,

(A.1b)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\eta_{3}^{[1]}:=\frac{-2{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{12}+3{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{8}+3}{16{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{8}(1+{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2})}\,,\qquad\eta_{3}^{[3]}:=\frac{-3{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{12}+9{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{8}-9{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}+27}{64{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{12}}\,,\\
&\psi_{3}^{[1]}:=\frac{2{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{12}-3{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{8}-3}{16{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{7}(1+{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2})}\,,\qquad\psi_{3}^{[3]}:=\frac{-9{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{12}+19{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{8}+5{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}+9}{64{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{13}}\,,\end{aligned}

(A.1c)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\eta_{4}^{[0]}:=\frac{-4{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{20}-4{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{18}+17{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{16}+6{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{14}-48{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{8}+6{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{6}+36{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}-9}{64{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{14}}\,,\\
&\eta_{4}^{[2]}:=\frac{1}{384{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{18}({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}+1)}\Big(-24{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{22}+285{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{18}+177{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{16}-862{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{14}-754{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{12}\\
&\qquad+1116{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{10}+1080{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{8}-162{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{6}-54{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}-81{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}-81\Big)\,,\\
&\eta_{4}^{[4]}:=\frac{21{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{20}+{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{16}-262{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{12}+522{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{8}+81{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}+405}{384{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{18}({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}+5)}\,,\\
&\psi_{4}^{[2]}:=\frac{1}{768{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{19}({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}+1)}\Big(-12{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{26}-36{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{24}+57{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{22}+93{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{20}+51{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{18}-21{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{16}-646{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{14}\,,\\
&\qquad-502{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{12}+1098{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{10}+1098{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{8}-243{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{6}-135{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}-81{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}-81\Big)\,,\\
&\psi_{4}^{[4]}:=\frac{-21{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{24}+60{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{20}+343{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{16}-1648{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{12}+3177{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{8}+756{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}+405}{1536{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{19}({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}+5)}\,,\end{aligned}

(A.1d)

\displaystyle c_{4}=\frac{1}{1024{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{19}({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}+1)}\Big(56{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{30}+88{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{28}-272{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{26}-528{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{24}-7{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{22}+497{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{20}+1917{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{18}

(A.1e)

\displaystyle\quad+1437{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{16}-4566{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{14}-4038{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{12}+4194{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{10}+3906{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{8}-891{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{6}-675{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}+81{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}+81\Big)\,.

The rest of this section is devoted to the proof of Proposition
A.1
.

In
[
10
, Theorem 2.1]
we have yet computed the second order expansion
of the
Stokes waves in (
1.9
) and proved that the coefficients

\eta_{2}^{[0]}
,
\eta_{2}^{[2]}
,
\psi_{2}^{[2]}

are (
A.1a
)
and
c_{2}
is (
A.1b
).
Then we write

\displaystyle\eta_{\epsilon}(x)=\epsilon\cos(x)+\epsilon^{2}(\eta_{2}^{[0]}+\eta_{2}^{[2]}\cos(2x))+\epsilon^{3}\eta_{3}(x)+\epsilon^{4}\eta_{4}(x)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{5})\,,

(A.2)

\displaystyle\psi_{\epsilon}(x)=\epsilon{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-1}\sin(x)+\epsilon^{2}\psi_{2}^{[2]}\sin(2x)+\epsilon^{3}\psi_{3}(x)+\epsilon^{4}\psi_{4}(x)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{5})\,,

\displaystyle c_{\epsilon}={\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}+\epsilon^{2}c_{2}+\epsilon^{3}c_{3}+\epsilon^{4}c_{4}+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{5})\,,

where the functions

\eta_{3}(x)
,
\eta_{4}(x)
are even and
\psi_{3}(x)
,
\psi_{4}(x)
are odd.
We rewrite the equations (
1.8
) as the system

\begin{cases}\eta-c\,\psi_{x}+\dfrac{\psi_{x}^{2}}{2}-\dfrac{\eta_{x}^{2}}{2(1+\eta_{x}^{2})}(c-\psi_{x})^{2}=0\\
c\,\eta_{x}+G(\eta)\psi=0\,,\end{cases}

(A.3)

where in the first equation we have replaced
G(\eta)\psi
with
-c\,\eta_{x}
.

We Taylor expand
the Dirichlet-Neumann operator
G(\eta)
as

G(\eta)=G_{0}+G_{1}(\eta)+G_{2}(\eta)+G_{3}(\eta)+\mathcal{O}(\eta^{4})

where, by
[
16
, formulae (39)-(40)]
,

\displaystyle

\displaystyle G_{0}:=D\tanh(\mathtt{h}D)=|D|\tanh(\mathtt{h}|D|)\,,

(A.4)

\displaystyle G_{1}(\eta):=-\partial_{x}\eta\partial_{x}-G_{0}\eta G_{0}\,,

\displaystyle G_{2}(\eta):=-\frac{1}{2}G_{0}\partial_{x}\eta^{2}\partial_{x}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{x}^{2}\eta^{2}G_{0}-G_{0}\eta G_{1}(\eta)\,,

\displaystyle G_{3}(\eta):=\frac{1}{6}\partial_{x}^{3}\eta^{3}\partial_{x}+\frac{1}{6}G_{0}\partial_{x}^{2}\eta^{3}G_{0}-G_{0}\eta G_{2}(\eta)+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{x}^{2}\eta^{2}G_{1}(\eta)

\displaystyle G_{4}(\eta):=\frac{1}{24}G_{0}\partial_{x}^{3}\eta^{4}\partial_{x}-\frac{1}{24}\partial_{x}^{4}\eta^{4}G_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{x}^{2}\eta^{2}G_{2}(\eta)+\frac{1}{6}G_{0}\partial_{x}^{2}\eta^{3}G_{1}(\eta)-G_{0}\eta G_{3}(\eta)\,.

Remark A.2
.

In order to check that (
A.4
) coincides with

[
16
, formulae (39)-(40)]
use the identity
D^{2}=-\partial_{x}^{2}
.
We point out that (
A.4
) coincides with
[
17
, formulae (2.13)-(2.14)]
and the recursion formulae of
[
50
, p. 24]
.

The ‘unperturbed" linear part of the system (
A.3
)
is associated with the self-adjoint closed operator

{\cal B}_{0}=\begin{bmatrix}1&-{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\partial_{x}\\
{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\partial_{x}&G_{0}\end{bmatrix}:\mathcal{D}\subset H^{\sigma,s}_{ev}\times H^{\sigma,s}_{odd}\longrightarrow H^{\sigma,s}_{ev}\times H^{\sigma,s}_{odd}\,,

(A.5)

with domain
\mathcal{D}:=H^{\sigma,s+1}_{ev}\times H^{\sigma,s+1}_{odd}
.
To compute the higher-order expansions one needs the following

Lemma A.3
.

The kernel of the operator
{\cal B}_{0}
in (
A.5
) is

K:=\text{Ker}\;{\cal B}_{0}=\text{span}\,\Big\{\begin{bmatrix}\cos(x)\\
{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-1}\sin(x)\end{bmatrix}\Big\}

(A.6a)

and its range
R:=\text{Rn}\;{\cal B}_{0}=K^{\perp_{\mathcal{D}}}
is given by
R=R_{0}\oplus R_{1}\oplus R_{\varnothing}
, where

\displaystyle R_{0}:=\text{span}\,\Big\{\begin{bmatrix}1\\
0\end{bmatrix}\Big\}\,,\ \ R_{1}:=\text{span}\,\Big\{\begin{bmatrix}-\cos(x)\\
{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\sin(x)\end{bmatrix}\Big\}\,,

(A.6b)

\displaystyle R_{\varnothing}:=\overline{\bigoplus_{k=2}^{\infty}R_{k}}\,,\ \ R_{k}:=\text{span}\,\Big\{\begin{bmatrix}\cos(kx)\\
0\end{bmatrix}\,,\;\begin{bmatrix}0\\
\sin(kx)\end{bmatrix}\Big\}\,.

Consequently there exists a unique self-adjoint bounded linear operator
{\cal B}_{0}^{-1}:R\to R
, given by

\displaystyle{\cal B}_{0}^{-1}\begin{bmatrix}1\\
0\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}1\\
0\end{bmatrix}\,,\quad{\cal B}_{0}^{-1}\begin{bmatrix}-\cos(x)\\
{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\sin(x)\end{bmatrix}=\frac{1}{1+{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}}\begin{bmatrix}-\cos(x)\\
{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\sin(x)\end{bmatrix}\,,

(A.7)

\displaystyle{\cal B}_{0}^{-1}\begin{bmatrix}f(x)\\
g(x)\end{bmatrix}=\big(|D|\tanh(\mathtt{h}|D|)+{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}\partial_{x}^{2}\big)^{-1}\begin{bmatrix}|D|\tanh(\mathtt{h}|D|)&{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\partial_{x}\\
-{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\partial_{x}&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}f(x)\\
g(x)\end{bmatrix}\,,\ \forall\begin{bmatrix}f\\
g\end{bmatrix}\in R_{\varnothing}\,,

such that
{\cal B}_{0}{\cal B}_{0}^{-1}={\cal B}_{0}^{-1}{\left.\kern-1.2pt{\cal B}_{0}\vphantom{\big|}\right|_{R}}=\mathrm{Id}_{R}
.

Proof.

This is the content of
[
10
, Lemma B.1]
.
Then (
A.7
) follows by inspection.
∎

From the second-order expansion of the Stokes waves in
[
10
, Appendix B]
we recover

G_{0}\psi_{1}={\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\sin(x)\,,\quad G_{1}(\eta_{1})\psi_{1}=\frac{1-{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}}{{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}(1+{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4})}\sin(2x)\,.

(A.8)

Third order in
\epsilon
.

\displaystyle G_{1}(\eta_{2})\psi_{1}=\frac{1}{{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}}\big(\eta_{2}^{[0]}(1-{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4})+\frac{1}{2}\eta_{2}^{[2]}(1+{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4})\big)\sin(x)+\frac{3}{2{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}}\eta_{2}^{[2]}\frac{1-{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{8}}{1+3{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}}\sin(3x)\,.

(which corresponds to a Galilean reference frame).

\displaystyle{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}+p_{\epsilon}(x):

\displaystyle=\displaystyle{\frac{c_{\epsilon}-V(x+\mathfrak{p}(x))}{1+\mathfrak{p}_{x}(x)}}\,,

\displaystyle 1+a_{\epsilon}(x):

\displaystyle=\displaystyle{\frac{1+(V(x+\mathfrak{p}(x))-c_{\epsilon})B_{x}(x+\mathfrak{p}(x))}{1+\mathfrak{p}_{x}(x)}}=\dfrac{1}{1+\mathfrak{p}_{x}(x)}-({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}+p_{\epsilon}(x))B_{x}(x+\mathfrak{p}(x))\,,

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\eta_{3}^{[1]}+B_{2}^{[2]}\eta_{2}^{[2]}+\frac{1}{2}B_{3}^{[1]}+\big(4\psi_{4}^{[4]}-\frac{3}{2}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\eta_{3}^{[3]}-B_{2}^{[2]}\eta_{2}^{[2]}-\frac{1}{2}B_{3}^{[3]}\big)\cos(4x)

From (

Appendix B
Expansion of the projector
P_{\mu,\epsilon}

In this Appendix we prove Lemmata
3.4
and
3.5
.

We denote, for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle

\displaystyle f_{k}^{+}:=\begin{bmatrix}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{1/2}\cos(kx)\\
{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-1/2}\sin(kx)\end{bmatrix}\,,\quad f_{k}^{-}:=\begin{bmatrix}-{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{1/2}\sin(kx)\\
{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-1/2}\cos(kx)\end{bmatrix}\,,

(B.1)

\displaystyle f_{-k}^{+}:=\begin{bmatrix}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{1/2}\cos(kx)\\
-{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-1/2}\sin(kx)\end{bmatrix}\,,\quad f_{-k}^{-}:=\begin{bmatrix}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{1/2}\sin(kx)\\
{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-1/2}\cos(kx)\end{bmatrix}\,,

and we define for any
k\in\mathbb{Z}
the spaces

\mathcal{W}_{k}:=\text{span}\left\{f_{k}^{+},\ f_{k}^{-},f_{-k}^{+},\ f_{-k}^{-}\right\}\,,\quad\mathcal{W}_{k}^{\sigma}:=\text{span}_{\mathbb{R}}\{f_{k}^{\sigma},\ f_{-k}^{\sigma}\}\,,\quad\sigma=\pm\,.

(B.2)

We have the following

Lemma B.1
.

The jets of the operator
{\cal B}_{\mu,\epsilon}
in (
3.8
) act on the spaces in (
B.2
) as follows
2
2

2

the sum is direct if
j\leq k
, otherwise some spaces may overlap.

{\cal B}_{\ell,j}\mathcal{W}_{k}^{\sigma}=\underbrace{\mathrm{i}\,^{\ell}\mathcal{W}_{k-j}^{(-1)^{\ell}\sigma}+_{\mathbb{R}}\mathrm{i}\,^{\ell}\mathcal{W}_{k-j+2}^{(-1)^{\ell}\sigma}+_{\mathbb{R}}\dots+_{\mathbb{R}}\mathrm{i}\,^{\ell}\mathcal{W}_{k+j}^{(-1)^{\ell}\sigma}}_{j+1\ terms}\,,\quad f_{0}^{-}\notin{\cal B}_{0,j}\mathcal{W}_{k}^{-}\,,

(B.3)

with
\ell,j=0,\dots,4
, while the operator
\mathcal{J}
in (
1.5
) acts as

\mathcal{J}\mathcal{W}_{k}^{\pm}=\mathcal{W}_{k}^{\mp}
.

Proof.

The first formula in (
B.3
) follows by (
3.8
)-(
3.9
).
Let us prove the second statement by contradiction supposing
that there exists
g\in\mathcal{W}_{k}^{-}
such that
{\cal B}_{0,j}g=f_{0}^{-}
. Then

1=\big(f_{0}^{-},f_{0}^{-}\big)=\big({\cal B}_{0,j}g,f_{0}^{-}\big)=\big(g,{\cal B}_{0,j}f_{0}^{-}\big)=0
, by (
3.8
), which is a contradiction.
∎

We now include an extended version of
[
10
, Lemma A.2]
.

Lemma B.2
.

The space
H^{1}(\mathbb{T})
decomposes as

H^{1}(\mathbb{T})=\mathcal{V}_{0,0}\oplus\mathcal{U}\oplus{\mathcal{W}_{H^{1}}}
, with
\mathcal{W}_{H^{1}}=\overline{\bigoplus\limits_{k\geq 2}^{{\color[rgb]{1,1,1},}}\mathcal{W}_{k}}^{{}_{\footnotesize\begin{matrix}H^{1}\end{matrix}}}

where the subspaces
\mathcal{V}_{0,0},\mathcal{U}
and
\mathcal{W}_{k}
, defined below, are
invariant under
\mathscr{L}_{0,0}
and the following properties hold:

(i)

\mathcal{V}_{0,0}=\text{span}\{f^{+}_{1},f^{-}_{1},f^{+}_{0},f^{-}_{0}\}
is the
generalized kernel of
\mathscr{L}_{0,0}
. For any
\lambda\neq 0
the operator

\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda:\mathcal{V}_{0,0}\to\mathcal{V}_{0,0}
is invertible and

\displaystyle(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}f_{1}^{+}=-\frac{1}{\lambda}f_{1}^{+}\,,\ (\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}f_{1}^{-}=-\frac{1}{\lambda}f_{1}^{-},\ (\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}f_{0}^{-}=-\frac{1}{\lambda}f_{0}^{-}\,,

(B.4a)

\displaystyle(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}f_{0}^{+}=-\frac{1}{\lambda}f_{0}^{+}+\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}f_{0}^{-}\,.

(B.4b)

(ii)

\mathcal{U}:=\text{span}\left\{f_{-1}^{+},f_{-1}^{-}\right\}
. For any

\lambda\neq\pm 2\mathrm{i}\,
the operator

\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda:\mathcal{U}\to\mathcal{U}
is invertible and

\displaystyle(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}f_{-1}^{+}=\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}+4{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}}\left(-\lambda f_{-1}^{+}+2{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}f_{-1}^{-}\right),

(B.4c)

\displaystyle(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}f_{-1}^{-}=\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}+4{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}}\left(-2{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}f_{-1}^{+}-\lambda f_{-1}^{-}\right)\,.

(iii)

Each
subspace
\mathcal{W}_{k}
in (
B.2
) is invariant under
\mathscr{L}_{0,0}
.
For any

|\lambda|<\delta(\mathtt{h})
small enough and any natural
k\geq 2
, the operator

{\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda:\mathcal{W}_{k}\to\mathcal{W}_{k}}

is invertible and for any
f\in\mathcal{W}_{k}
and any natural number
N

\displaystyle(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}f=\mathscr{L}_{0,0}^{\,\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\perp$}}f+\lambda\big(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}^{\,\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\perp$}}\big)^{2}f+\dots+\lambda^{N-1}\big(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}^{\,\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\perp$}}\big)^{N}f+\lambda^{N}\varphi_{f,N}(\lambda,x)\,,

(B.4d)

for some analytic function
\lambda\mapsto\varphi_{f,N}(\lambda,\cdot)\in\mathcal{W}_{k}
, where
\mathscr{L}_{0,0}^{\,\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\perp$}}:\mathcal{W}_{k}\to\mathcal{W}_{k}
is

{\footnotesize\mathscr{L}_{0,0}^{\,\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\perp$}}:=\begin{matrix}\big({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}\partial_{x}^{2}+|D|\tanh(\mathtt{h}|D|)\big)^{-1}\end{matrix}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\partial_{x}&-|D|\tanh(\mathtt{h}|D|)\\
1&{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\partial_{x}\end{bmatrix},\hskip 8.50012pt\mathscr{L}_{0,0}^{\,\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\perp$}}\mathcal{W}^{\pm}_{k}=\mathcal{W}^{\mp}_{k}\,.}

(B.5)

Remark B.3
.

We will use in the sequel the following
decomposition formula, for any,

\begin{aligned} &{\footnotesize\begin{matrix}\begin{bmatrix}\mathtt{a}\cos(x)\\
\mathtt{b}\sin(x)\end{bmatrix}\end{matrix}}=\frac{1}{2}({\mathtt{a}}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}+{\mathtt{b}}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{\frac{1}{2}})f_{1}^{+}+\frac{1}{2}({\mathtt{a}}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}-{\mathtt{b}}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{\frac{1}{2}})f_{-1}^{+}\,,\\
&{\footnotesize\begin{matrix}\begin{bmatrix}\mathtt{a}\sin(x)\\
\mathtt{b}\cos(x)\end{bmatrix}\end{matrix}}=\frac{1}{2}({\mathtt{b}}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{\frac{1}{2}}-{\mathtt{a}}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-\frac{1}{2}})f_{1}^{-}+\frac{1}{2}({\mathtt{b}}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{\frac{1}{2}}+{\mathtt{a}}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-\frac{1}{2}})f_{-1}^{-}\,,\end{aligned}\quad\forall{\mathtt{a}}\,,{\mathtt{b}}\in\mathbb{C}\,.

(B.6)

Notation.
We denote by
\mathcal{O}(\lambda)

an analytic function having a zero of order
1
at
\lambda=0
and

\mathcal{O}_{Z}(\lambda^{m})

an analytic function with valued in a subspace
Z

having a zero of order
m
at
\lambda=0
.
We denote with
\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{-1}:\lambda)
any function having a Laurent series at
\lambda=0
of the form

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}\end{subarray}}a_{j}\lambda^{j}
. We denote by
f_{{\cal W}_{k}}

a function in
{\cal W}_{k}
.

If
h(\lambda)=h_{0}+\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{-1}:\lambda)
,
h_{0}\in\mathbb{C}
, then, by the residue theorem,

\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}\,}\oint_{\Gamma}\frac{h(\lambda)}{\lambda}\mathrm{d}\lambda=h_{0}\,.

(B.7)

We prepend to the proof of Lemma
3.4
a list of results given by straight-forward computations.

&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\lambda^{2}f_{\mathcal{W}_{1}^{-}}+\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}_{3}}(\lambda^{2})+\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{3})\,,\end{aligned}

(B.19)

\displaystyle[(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{0,1}]^{2}f_{0}^{+}=\zeta_{0}^{+}\mathrm{B}_{2}^{+}+\frac{\alpha_{0}^{+}}{4{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}}\mathrm{A}_{-2}^{+}+\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{-1}:\lambda)\,,\ \ [(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{0,1}]^{2}f_{0}^{-}=0\,.

Proof.

Apply twice Lemma
B.4
and use that

\displaystyle\lambda^{2}\,(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{0,1}\,f_{\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\pm}}=\lambda\alpha_{5}^{\pm}f_{1}^{\mp}+\lambda^{2}f_{\mathcal{W}_{1}^{\pm}}+\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}_{3}}(\lambda^{2})+\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{3})\ ,

\displaystyle\lambda^{3}\,(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{0,1}\ f_{\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\mp}}=\lambda^{2}\alpha_{6}^{\pm}f_{1}^{\pm}+\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{3})\ ,

\displaystyle(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{0,1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}_{2}}(\lambda^{4})=\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{3})

where
\alpha_{5}^{\pm}
,
\alpha_{6}^{\pm}
are real numbers.
∎

We further list a series of identities to exploit later.

By applying first Lemma
B.5
and then Lemma
B.4
we get

\displaystyle(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{0,1}(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{1,0}f_{1}^{+}=-\mathrm{i}\,\mu_{\mathtt{h}}\Big[\frac{\zeta_{1}^{+}}{\lambda^{2}}f_{0}^{-}+\frac{1}{\lambda}\mathrm{A}_{2}^{+}+f_{\mathcal{W}_{2}^{-}}+\alpha_{7}f_{0}^{-}+\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}_{2}}(\lambda)+\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}_{0}}(\lambda^{2})\Big]\,,

\displaystyle{(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{0,1}(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{1,0}f_{1}^{-}=-\mathrm{i}\,\mu_{\mathtt{h}}\Big[\frac{1}{\lambda}\Big(\mathrm{A}_{2}^{-}-\frac{2{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\zeta_{-1}^{+}}{\lambda^{2}+4{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}}f_{0}^{-}\Big)+\mathrm{J}_{2}^{+}+\lambda f_{\mathcal{W}_{2}^{-}}+\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}_{2}}(\lambda^{2})}\Big]\,,

\displaystyle(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{0,1}(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{1,0}f_{0}^{\pm}=0\,,

(B.20)

where
\alpha_{7}
is a real number and

\mathrm{J}_{2}^{+}(x):=\mathrm{B}_{2}^{+}(x)-\frac{1}{2{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}}\mathrm{A}_{-2}^{+}(x)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{risolventeintermedio.1}}}{{=}}\begin{bmatrix}\dfrac{(5{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}+4)p_{1}^{[1]}-a_{1}^{[1]}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}+2)}{4{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{19/2}}\cos(2x)\\[11.38109pt]
\dfrac{({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}+1)\big((3{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}+4)p_{1}^{[1]}-2a_{1}^{[1]}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\big)}{8{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{21/2}}\sin(2x)\end{bmatrix}\,.

(B.21)

By applying first Lemma
B.4
and then Lemma
B.5
, and since
\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{1,0}\mathcal{W}_{k}\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{k}
, we get

\displaystyle(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{1,0}(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{0,1}f_{1}^{+}=\mathrm{i}\,f_{\mathcal{W}_{2}^{-}}+\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}_{2}}(\lambda)\,,

(B.22)

\displaystyle(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{1,0}(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{0,1}f_{1}^{-}=\mathrm{i}\,\mathrm{S}_{2}^{+}+\mathrm{i}\,\lambda f_{\mathcal{W}_{2}^{-}}+\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}_{2}}(\lambda^{2})\,,

\displaystyle(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{1,0}(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{0,1}f_{0}^{+}=\mathrm{i}\,f_{\mathcal{W}_{1}^{-}}+\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{-1}:\lambda)\,,

\displaystyle(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{1,0}(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{0,1}f_{0}^{-}=0\,,

where, using also (
B.5
), (
3.8b
)–(
3.10a
), (
B.9
)

\mathrm{i}\,\mathrm{S}_{2}^{+}(x):=(\mathscr{L}_{0,0})^{\,\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\perp$}}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{1,0}\mathrm{A}_{2}^{-}=\mathrm{i}\,\begin{bmatrix}\dfrac{({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{8}\mathtt{h}+{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{6}-2{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}\mathtt{h}+{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}+\mathtt{h})(a_{1}^{[1]}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}-2p_{1}^{[1]})}{4{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{21/2}}\cos(2x)\\[11.38109pt]
\dfrac{({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{8}\mathtt{h}+{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{6}-2{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}\mathtt{h}+{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}+\mathtt{h})(a_{1}^{[1]}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}-2p_{1}^{[1]})}{8{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{23/2}}\sin(2x)\end{bmatrix}\,.

(B.23)

We are now in position to prove Lemmata
3.4
and
3.5
.

Proof of Lemma
3.4
.
The proof is divided in three parts, one for each group of formulas in (
3.19
).

Computation of
P_{0,2}f_{j}^{\sigma}
.
Since
P_{0,\epsilon}f_{0}^{-}=f_{0}^{-}
(cfr.
[
10
, Lemma A.4]
) we have

P_{0,2}f_{0}^{-}=0\,.

(B.24)

On the other hand, for
f_{j}^{\sigma}\in\{f_{1}^{+},f_{1}^{-},f_{0}^{+}\}
,
in view of (
3.13
) we have, by (
B.4a
)-(
B.4b
) and since
{\cal B}_{0,1}f_{0}^{-}={\cal B}_{0,2}f_{0}^{-}=0
,

\displaystyle P_{0,2}f_{j}^{\sigma}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}\,}\oint_{\Gamma}\frac{(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}}{\lambda}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{0,2}f_{j}^{\sigma}\mathrm{d}\lambda

(B.25)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}\,}\oint_{\Gamma}\frac{(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}}{\lambda}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{0,1}(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{0,1}f_{j}^{\sigma}\mathrm{d}\lambda=:\mathrm{I}_{j}^{\sigma}+\mathrm{I\!I}_{j}^{\sigma}\,.

In case

one readily sees, in view of (
B.14
) for
\mathrm{I}_{0}^{+}
and (
B.19
) for
\mathrm{I\!I}_{0}^{+}
, that
P_{0,2}f_{0}^{+}\in\mathcal{W}_{2}^{+}
which implies the second statement in (
3.19a
).
We now compute the remaining four terms.

First by Lemma
B.6
and the residue theorem

\mathrm{I}_{1}^{+}=-\frac{\ell_{1}^{+}}{{4{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}}}f_{-1}^{+}-\mathrm{L}_{3}^{+}\,,\qquad\mathrm{I}_{1}^{-}=-\frac{\ell_{1}^{-}}{{4{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}}}f_{-1}^{-}+f_{\mathcal{W}_{3}^{-}}\,.

(B.26)

Then
by (
B.19
) and the residue theorem

\mathrm{I\!I}_{1}^{+}=\frac{\alpha_{2}^{+}}{4{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}}f_{-1}^{+}+\zeta_{3}^{+}f_{1}^{+}+\mathrm{A}_{3}^{+}\,,\qquad\mathrm{I\!I}_{1}^{-}=\frac{\alpha_{2}^{-}}{4{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}}f_{-1}^{-}+\zeta_{3}^{-}f_{1}^{-}+f_{\mathcal{W}_{3}^{-}}\ .

(B.27)

In conclusion we have formulae (
3.19a
), (
3.19b
) with

P_{0,2}f_{1}^{+}=\underbrace{\frac{\alpha_{2}^{+}-\ell_{1}^{+}}{4{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}}}_{\mathfrak{u}_{0,2}^{+}}f_{-1}^{+}+\underbrace{\zeta_{3}^{+}}_{\mathfrak{n}_{0,2}}f_{1}^{+}\underbrace{-\mathrm{L}_{3}^{+}+\mathrm{A}_{3}^{+}}_{\scriptsize\begin{bmatrix}\mathfrak{a}_{0,2}\cos(3x)\\
\mathfrak{b}_{0,2}\sin(3x)\end{bmatrix}},\quad P_{0,2}f_{1}^{-}=\underbrace{\frac{\alpha_{2}^{-}-\ell_{1}^{-}}{4{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}}}_{\mathfrak{u}_{0,2}^{-}}f_{-1}^{-}+\underbrace{\zeta_{3}^{-}}_{\mathfrak{n}_{0,2}}f_{1}^{-}+f_{\mathcal{W}_{3}^{-}}\,,

and we obtain the explicit expression (
3.19c
) of the coefficients given by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{n}_{0,2}:=\zeta_{3}^{+}\,,\ \ \mathfrak{u}_{0,2}^{+}:=\frac{\alpha_{2}^{+}-\ell_{1}^{+}}{4{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}}\,,\ \ \mathfrak{u}_{0,2}^{-}:=\frac{\alpha_{2}^{-}-\ell_{1}^{-}}{4{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}}\,,\ \ {\footnotesize\begin{bmatrix}\mathfrak{a}_{0,2}\cos(3x)\\
\mathfrak{b}_{0,2}\sin(3x)\end{bmatrix}}:=\mathrm{A}_{3}^{+}(x)-\mathrm{L}_{3}^{+}(x)\,,

with
\zeta_{3}^{+}
,
\alpha_{2}^{\pm}
,

Computation of
P_{2,0}f_{0}^{-}
. Since

[
10

(B.28)

Computation of

.
In case

and

We now compute the remaining terms.
By Lemma
B.7
and the residue theorem

\mathrm{I\!I\!I}_{1}^{-}=-{\mathrm{i}\,\mathrm{Q}_{2}^{+}}\,,\quad\mathrm{I\!I\!I}_{0}^{-}=-\frac{\mathrm{i}\,}{2{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{3/2}}f_{-1}^{+}\,.

(B.31)

In conclusion we have formulae (

. ∎

\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{0,1}{\mathrm{L}_{3}^{+}}={\mathrm{Y}_{2}^{-}}+f_{\mathcal{W}_{4}^{-}}\,,

(B.43a)

{\mathrm{Y}_{2}^{-}}(x):=\begin{bmatrix}\dfrac{p_{1}^{[1]}\big(a_{2}^{[2]}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}(3+{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4})-2(3+5{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4})p_{2}^{[2]}\big)}{16{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{9/2}}\sin(2x)\\[11.38109pt]
-\dfrac{3(1+3{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4})p_{1}^{[1]}(a_{2}^{[2]}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}-2p_{2}^{[2]})+a_{1}^{[1]}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\big(-a_{2}^{[2]}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}(3+{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4})+2(3+5{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4})p_{2}^{[2]}\big)}{32{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{11/2}}\cos(2x)\end{bmatrix}\,.

(B.43b)

In view of (
B.3
) and Lemma

, one obtains by inspection

\frac{\lambda m_{1}^{+}}{\lambda^{2}+4{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}}(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{0,1}f_{-1}^{-}=\mathcal{O}(\lambda)\,,\quad(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{0,1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}_{3}}(\lambda)=\mathcal{O}(\lambda)\,.

Then (

), Lemmata

,

{\footnotesize\begin{matrix}(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{0,1}(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{0,2}f_{1}^{+}\end{matrix}}=\tau_{1}^{+}{\mathrm{B}_{2}^{+}}+\frac{\ell_{1}^{+}}{4\text{${\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}$}}{\mathrm{A}_{-2}^{+}}+\mathscr{L}_{0,0}^{\,\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\perp$}}({\mathrm{Y}_{2}^{-}}+f_{\mathcal{W}_{4}^{-}})+\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{-1}:\lambda)\,,

and, by (
B.7
), the term
\mathrm{V\!I\!I\!I}
in (

\mathrm{V\!I\!I\!I}=\tau_{1}^{+}{\mathrm{B}_{2}^{+}}+\frac{\ell_{1}^{+}}{4{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}}{\mathrm{A}_{-2}^{+}}+\mathscr{L}_{0,0}^{\,\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\perp$}}{\mathrm{Y}_{2}^{-}}+\widetilde{f}_{\mathcal{W}_{4}^{+}}\,,

(B.44)

where

\mathscr{L}_{0,0}^{\,\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\perp$}}{\mathrm{Y}_{2}^{-}}(x)=\begin{bmatrix}\kappa_{3}\cos(2x)\\[11.38109pt]
\varsigma_{3}\sin(2x)\end{bmatrix}\,.

(B.45)

with

\displaystyle\kappa_{3}:

\displaystyle=\dfrac{a_{1}^{[1]}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\big(a_{2}^{[2]}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}+3)-2(5{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}+3)p_{2}^{[2]}\big)-a_{2}^{[2]}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{8}+13{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}+6)p_{1}^{[1]}+2({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}+3)(5{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}+2)p_{1}^{[1]}p_{2}^{[2]}}{32{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{19/2}}\,,

\displaystyle\varsigma_{3}:

\displaystyle=\dfrac{({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}+1)\big[a_{1}^{[1]}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}\big(a_{2}^{[2]}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}+3)-2(5{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}+3)p_{2}^{[2]}\big)-2a_{2}^{[2]}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}(5{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}+3)p_{1}^{[1]}+4(7{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}+3)p_{1}^{[1]}p_{2}^{[2]}\big]}{64{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{21/2}}\,.

\mathrm{I\!X}\big)
By (
B.19
) we get

\displaystyle[(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{0,1}]^{3}f_{1}^{+}=\frac{\zeta_{2}^{+}}{\lambda}(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{0,1}f_{1}^{-}

(B.46a)

\displaystyle+\frac{\alpha_{2}^{+}}{\lambda^{2}+4{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}}(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{0,1}f_{-1}^{+}+\zeta_{3}^{+}(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{0,1}f_{1}^{+}+(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{0,1}{\mathrm{A}_{3}^{+}}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &+\lambda(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{0,1}\left(f_{\mathcal{W}_{1}^{-}}+f_{\mathcal{W}_{3}^{-}}\right)+\lambda^{2}(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{0,1}f_{\mathcal{W}_{1}^{+}}\\
&+(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{0,1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}_{3}}(\lambda^{2})+(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{0,1}\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{3})\,.\end{aligned}

the terms in the two lines in (

\displaystyle\ (\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{0,1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}_{3}}(\lambda^{2})=\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{2})\,,\quad(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{0,1}\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{3})=\mathcal{O}(\lambda)\,.

The remaining terms in (

\displaystyle[(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{0,1}]^{3}f_{1}^{+}=\zeta_{2}^{+}{\mathrm{B}_{2}^{+}}+\frac{\alpha_{2}^{+}}{4\text{${\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}$}}{\mathrm{A}_{-2}^{+}}+\zeta_{3}^{+}\mathrm{A}_{2}^{+}+\mathscr{L}_{0,0}^{\,\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\perp$}}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{0,1}{\mathrm{A}_{3}^{+}}+\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{-1}:\lambda)\,.

By

\frac{(a_{1}^{[1]}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}-p_{1}^{[1]})\left((a_{1}^{[1]})^{2}\left({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}+3\right){\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}-2a_{1}^{[1]}p_{1}^{[1]}\left({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{8}+13{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}+6\right){\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}+3(p_{1}^{[1]})^{2}\left(9{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{8}+15{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}+4\right)\right)}{64{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{21/2}}\cos(2x)\end{bmatrix}\,.

(B.49)

Finally, by (

) and (

), we get that the term
\mathrm{I\!X}
in (

\displaystyle\kappa_{4}:=\frac{1}{64{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{29/2}}\big(-(a_{1}^{[1]})^{3}({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}+3){\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{3}+(a_{1}^{[1]})^{2}p_{1}^{[1]}(3{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{8}+31{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}+18){\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}

\displaystyle-a_{1}^{[1]}(p_{1}^{[1]})^{2}(2{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{12}+49{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{8}+101{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}+36){\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}+(p_{1}^{[1]})^{3}(11{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{12}+67{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{8}+86{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}+24)\big)

\displaystyle\varsigma_{4}:=\frac{({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}+1)}{{128{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{31/2}}}\big(-(a_{1}^{[1]})^{3}({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}+3){\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{3}+2(a_{1}^{[1]})^{2}p_{1}^{[1]}({\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{8}+14{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}+9){\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}

\displaystyle-a_{1}^{[1]}(p_{1}^{[1]})^{2}(31{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{8}+89{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}+36){\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}+2(p_{1}^{[1]})^{3}(19{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{8}+37{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}+12)\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+(\tau_{1}^{+}-\zeta_{2}^{+})\mathrm{B}_{2}^{+}(x)+\frac{\ell_{1}^{+}-\alpha_{2}^{+}}{4{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{2}}\mathrm{A}_{-2}^{+}(x)-\zeta_{3}^{+}\mathrm{A}_{2}^{+}(x)\,,

Computation of
P_{1,2}f_{0}^{-}
.
By (

), (

reduces to

\displaystyle P_{1,2}f_{0}^{-}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}\,}\oint_{\Gamma}\frac{(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}}{\lambda}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{1,2}f_{0}^{-}\mathrm{d}\lambda

\displaystyle\ +\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}\,}\oint_{\Gamma}\frac{(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}}{\lambda}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{0,1}(\mathscr{L}_{0,0}-\lambda)^{-1}\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{1,1}f_{0}^{-}\mathrm{d}\lambda=:\mathrm{X}+\mathrm{X\!I}\,.

(B.52)

We now compute the two terms.

\mathrm{X}\big)
By (
3.46
) we have

\mathcal{J}{\cal B}_{1,2}f_{0}^{-}=\mathrm{i}\,a_{3}f_{0}^{-}+{\mathrm{i}\,\mathrm{W}_{-2}^{-}}

\cos(2x)\end{bmatrix}}\,

\mathrm{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{residuethm}}}{{=}}-\mathrm{i}\,\mathscr{L}_{0,0}^{\,\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\perp$}}{\mathrm{W}_{-2}^{-}}\,,\quad\mathscr{L}_{0,0}^{\,\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\perp$}}{\mathrm{W}_{-2}^{-}}(x)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{L0inv}}}{{=}}p_{2}^{[2]}\begin{bmatrix}{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{-4}\cos(2x)\\
\dfrac{1+{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{4}}{2{\mathtt{c}}_{\mathtt{h}}^{5}}\sin(2x)\end{bmatrix}\,.

\mathrm{X\!I}\big)

B.7
,
B.4
one has

and therefore

In conclusion, by (
